```
     1→# Wave Decisions: Intent-Gated MCP Tool Access
     2→
     3→Summary of design decisions for downstream waves (DISTILL, DELIVER, DEVOPS).
     4→
     5→---
     6→
     7→## For Acceptance Designer (DISTILL Wave)
     8→
     9→### Endpoint
    10→
    11→- Route: `POST /mcp/agent/:sessionName`
    12→- Auth: `X-Brain-Auth` proxy token (no DPoP)
    13→- Protocol: JSON-RPC 2.0 over HTTP (MCP protocol)
    14→
    15→### Three MCP Methods
    16→
    17→1. **tools/list**: Returns classified tool list (authorized + gated + brain-native)
    18→2. **tools/call**: Executes authorized tool calls via upstream MCP server
    19→3. **create_intent**: Brain-native tool for intent-based escalation
    20→
    21→### Key Behaviors to Test
    22→
    23→- Scope computation consistency: tools/list and tools/call use the same scope function
    24→- Gates edge direction: `intent -gates-> agent_session` (NOT session -> intent)
    25→- Effective scope: `can_use grants INTERSECT intent authorization_details`
    26→- Gated tool description enrichment: includes create_intent instructions
    27→- 403 `intent_required` with `action_spec_template` on unauthorized tools/call
    28→- 403 `constraint_violation` with field-level details on constraint breach
    29→- Trace record for every tools/call (success, error, timeout, rejected)
    30→- create_intent auto-approve path: intent created -> policy evaluated -> authorized -> gates edge created
    31→- create_intent veto path: intent created -> pending_veto -> gates edge created -> agent yields
    32→- Observer resume: authorized intent + idle session -> adapter.resumeSession
    33→
    34→### Walking Skeleton Scope
    35→
    36→US-01 (tools/list) + US-02 (tools/call) + US-03 (create_intent, auto-approve only). Human veto and observer resume are Release 1.
    37→
    38→---
    39→
    40→## For Software Crafter (DELIVER Wave)
    41→
    42→### Development Paradigm
    43→
    44→Functional (per CLAUDE.md). Apply:
    45→- Types-first: define algebraic data types before implementations
    46→- Composition pipelines: data flows through pure transformation chains
    47→- Pure core / effect shell: scope computation and tool classification are pure; DB queries and upstream calls at boundaries
    48→- Function signatures as ports: `QuerySessionIntents`, `QueryGrantedTools`, `LookupProxyToken` are injected
    49→- Immutable state: all types use `readonly`
    50→
    51→### Module Structure
    52→
    53→Seven new modules under `app/src/server/mcp/`:
    54→
    55→| Module | Type | Dependencies |
    56→|--------|------|-------------|
    57→| `agent-mcp-route.ts` | Effect boundary | All handlers, auth |
    58→| `agent-mcp-auth.ts` | Effect boundary | proxy-auth, SurrealDB |
    59→| `scope-engine.ts` | Pure core + effect shell | rar-verifier (pure), tool-resolver, SurrealDB |
    60→| `tools-list-handler.ts` | Pure | scope-engine types, error-builder |
    61→| `tools-call-handler.ts` | Effect boundary | scope-engine, rar-verifier, mcp-client, trace-writer |
    62→| `create-intent-handler.ts` | Effect boundary | intent-queries, authorizer, policy-gate |
    63→| `error-response-builder.ts` | Pure | None |
    64→
    65→### Key Reuse Points
    66→
    67→- `resolveProxyAuth` from `proxy/proxy-auth.ts` for token -> session resolution
    68→- `verifyOperationScope` from `oauth/rar-verifier.ts` for constraint enforcement (US-06)
    69→- `evaluateIntent` from `intent/authorizer.ts` for policy + LLM evaluation
    70→- `captureToolTrace` from `proxy/tool-trace-writer.ts` for trace recording
    71→- `McpClientFactory.connect` + `callTool` from `tool-registry/mcp-client.ts` for upstream forwarding
    72→- `resolveToolsForIdentity` from `proxy/tool-resolver.ts` for can_use grant resolution
    73→- `adapter.resumeSession` from `orchestrator/sandbox-adapter.ts` for observer resume
    74→
    75→### Gates Edge Direction
    76→
    77→Existing schema: `DEFINE TABLE gates TYPE RELATION IN intent OUT agent_session`. Use `RELATE $intent->gates->$sess`. Query: `SELECT in.* FROM gates WHERE out = $session`.
    78→
    79→### SurrealDB Protected Variables
    80→
    81→Use `$sess` not `$session` for bound parameters (SurrealDB v3.0 reserves `$session`).
    82→
    83→---
    84→
    85→## For Platform Architect (DEVOPS Wave)
    86→
    87→### Route Registration
    88→
    89→New route `POST /mcp/agent/:sessionName` registered in `start-server.ts`. No new process, no new service.
    90→
    91→### Schema Migration
    92→
    93→None required. All tables exist.
    94→
    95→### Observability
    96→
    97→- OTel span via `withTracing` on the route handler
    98→- Span attributes: `mcp.method`, `mcp.tool_name`, `mcp.outcome`, `mcp.intent_id`, `session.id`, `workspace.id`
    99→- Trace records in SurrealDB for every tool call (audit trail)
   100→
   101→### External Integration Contract Tests
   102→
   103→Contract tests recommended for upstream MCP servers accessed via `McpClientFactory`. These are external integrations with the highest risk of breaking changes:
   104→- Consumer-driven contracts via **Pact-JS** (MIT) in CI acceptance stage
   105→- Priority: any upstream server where tool calls have financial or destructive side effects (e.g., Stripe, GitHub write operations)
   106→
   107→### Architectural Enforcement
   108→
   109→Recommended: `dependency-cruiser` (MIT) for import boundary validation in CI.
   110→
   111→---
   112→
   113→## Release Plan Summary
   114→
   115→| Release | Stories | Key Components | Dependencies |
   116→|---------|---------|---------------|-------------|
   117→| Walking Skeleton | US-01, US-02, US-03 | agent-mcp-route, auth, scope-engine, handlers | sandbox-agent-integration R2, mcp_tool registry |
   118→| R1: Yield-and-Resume | US-04, US-05 | Observer scan pattern, governance feed card | Walking Skeleton |
   119→| R2: Constraints + Composites | US-06, US-07 | RAR verifier integration, composite action_spec | Walking Skeleton |
   120→| R3: Hardening | US-08 | Scope cache, intent dedup, timeout handling | All above |
   121→
```

```
     1→# DISTILL Wave Decisions: Intent-Gated MCP Tool Access
     2→
     3→## Synthesis Summary
     4→
     5→### Test Framework Decision
     6→
     7→Bun's built-in test runner (`describe`, `it`, `expect` from `bun:test`) with the project's `acceptance-test-kit.ts` for in-process server boot and isolated SurrealDB namespace. No BDD frameworks. Gherkin scenarios from DISCUSS wave are translated into Bun test format with Given/When/Then comments.
     8→
     9→### Test File Organization
    10→
    11→Single test file: `tests/acceptance/agent-mcp-governance.test.ts`
    12→
    13→Rationale: All scenarios share the same server boot, test fixtures, and MCP endpoint helpers. The CI matrix auto-discovers directories under `tests/acceptance/`, so a single file in the root acceptance directory works. If the file grows past ~800 lines, split into `tests/acceptance/agent-mcp-governance/` directory.
    14→
    15→### Driving Port
    16→
    17→All tests exercise `POST /mcp/agent/:sessionName` via HTTP fetch. No internal module imports. Auth via `X-Brain-Auth` proxy token header. Test data setup via direct SurrealDB operations using `shared-fixtures.ts` helpers.
    18→
    19→### Test Data Strategy
    20→
    21→- Workspace + identity: `createWorkspaceDirectly()` (no HTTP auth needed for MCP proxy tests)
    22→- Agent session: direct `CREATE agent_session` in SurrealDB
    23→- Proxy token: `seedProxyToken()` from shared-fixtures, extended with `session` and `intent` fields
    24→- MCP tools: direct `CREATE mcp_tool` records
    25→- can_use grants: direct `RELATE identity->can_use->mcp_tool`
    26→- Intents: `createIntentDirectly()` from shared-fixtures with `authorization_details`
    27→- Gates edges: direct `RELATE intent->gates->agent_session`
    28→- Policies: direct `CREATE policy` records with tool-matching rules
    29→
    30→### Upstream MCP Server Mocking
    31→
    32→Tests need a mock upstream MCP server to verify forwarding. Two options considered:
    33→
    34→1. **MSW (Mock Service Worker)** -- intercepts fetch at network level. Good for HTTP-based MCP transport.
    35→2. **mcpClientFactoryOverride** -- injects a mock `McpClientFactory` via `AcceptanceSuiteOptions`.
    36→
    37→Decision: Use `mcpClientFactoryOverride` (option 2). It matches the existing pattern in `tool-registry-ui-test-kit.ts` and avoids MSW complexity for MCP protocol (which may use WebSocket transport).
    38→
    39→### Skip/Enable Strategy
    40→
    41→All scenarios except WS-1 start with `it.skip`. Implementation proceeds one-at-a-time per the walking-skeleton.md sequence. Each enabled scenario must pass before enabling the next.
    42→
    43→### Scope Computation Consistency (@property)
    44→
    45→The property that tools/list and tools/call use the same scope function is verified structurally: the test calls tools/list, sees a tool as authorized, then calls tools/call for the same tool and expects success. If scope computation diverges, the test fails.
    46→
    47→### Observer Resume Testing
    48→
    49→WS-3 (yield-and-resume) does NOT test the observer's graph scan directly. Instead, it tests the observable outcome: after human approval, the session can be resumed and the tool call succeeds. The observer's internal scan mechanism is an implementation detail tested at unit level.
    50→
    51→### Mandate Compliance Evidence
    52→
    53→- **CM-A**: Test file imports only from `bun:test`, `surrealdb`, `acceptance-test-kit`, and `shared-fixtures`. Zero internal component imports.
    54→- **CM-B**: Test descriptions use business language ("agent discovers tools", "agent escalates for gated tool"). Zero technical jargon in scenario names.
    55→- **CM-C**: 3 walking skeletons + 21 focused scenarios = correct ratio.
    56→
```

     1→# Architecture Design: Intent-Gated MCP Tool Access
     2→
     3→## System Overview
     4→
     5→Intent-gated MCP is a dynamic per-agent MCP endpoint that gates external tool calls behind intent authorization and policy evaluation. Sandbox coding agents (Claude Code, Codex) interact with Brain through this endpoint, which computes an effective tool scope from the agent's session-linked intents and either forwards authorized calls to upstream MCP servers or guides the agent through intent-based escalation.
     6→
     7→The endpoint is a new route module within Brain's existing modular monolith, composing existing infrastructure: proxy auth, tool resolver, intent lifecycle, policy gate, RAR verifier, MCP client factory, and trace writer.
     8→
     9→---

    79→## C4 Component (L3) -- Agent MCP Endpoint
    80→
    81→The Agent MCP Endpoint is the primary new subsystem with 6+ internal concerns, warranting a component diagram.

   120→
   121→---
   122→
   123→## Data Flow: Request Pipeline
   124→
   125→Every request to the dynamic MCP endpoint follows this pipeline:
   126→
   127→```
   128→Request -> proxy_auth -> session_resolution -> scope_computation -> handler_dispatch
   129→                                                                        |
   130→                                    +-----------------------------------+
   131→                                    |                |                  |
   132→                              tools/list        tools/call        create_intent
   133→                                    |                |                  |
   134→                              classify tools    scope check +       create intent +
   135→                              authorized vs     constraint          policy gate +
   136→                              gated             validation          gates edge
   137→                                    |                |                  |
   138→                              ListToolsResult   forward to          intent status
   139→                                                upstream MCP        response
   140→                                                    |
   141→                                              trace record
   142→```
   143→
   144→---
   145→
   146→## Key Design Decisions (Preserved)
   147→
   148→These decisions are carried forward from the DISCUSS wave and research. They are NOT new ADRs -- they are documented here for completeness.
   149→
   150→### D1: No DPoP for sandbox agents
   151→
   152→Sandbox agents authenticate via `X-Brain-Auth` proxy token only. The existing `proxy-auth.ts` already supports session and intent fields on `proxy_token`. No new auth mechanism needed.
   153→
   154→**Rationale**: Sandbox agents are opaque processes that receive env vars. They cannot generate DPoP proofs (which require a private key and proof JWT generation per request). The proxy token is simpler and sufficient -- it binds to a session and workspace.
   155→
   156→### D2: Proxy token with intent + session binding
   157→
   158→The `proxy_token` table already has `intent` and `session` fields (added in sandbox-agent-integration R2). The Agent MCP endpoint uses `session` to resolve gates edges and compute effective scope.
   159→
   160→### D3: Agent-driven escalation via create_intent tool
   161→
   162→Two escalation mechanisms:
   163→- **Proactive**: tools/list enriches gated tool descriptions with instructions to create an intent first
   164→- **Reactive**: tools/call returns structured 403 `{ code: -32403, data: { tool, action_spec_template } }`
   165→
   166→The agent calls `create_intent` MCP tool, which creates the intent, submits it through the policy gate, and returns the result.
   167→
   168→### D4: Yield-and-resume, not polling
   169→
   170→When `create_intent` returns `pending_veto`, the agent yields (session goes idle). The Observer detects the authorized intent and triggers `adapter.resumeSession`. The agent does NOT poll.
   171→
   172→### D5: Intent accumulation via gates edges
   173→
   174→A session accumulates intents over its lifetime. The `gates` relation (`intent -> agent_session`, per existing schema) links intents to sessions. Scope computation unions all authorized intents' `authorization_details`.
   175→
   176→**Schema note**: The existing `gates` table is defined as `IN intent OUT agent_session`. This means the relation direction is `intent -gates-> agent_session`. Scope queries traverse: `SELECT in.* FROM gates WHERE out = $session AND in.status = "authorized"`.
   177→
   178→### D6: Effective scope = can_use grants INTERSECT intent authorization_details
   179→
   180→A tool must be BOTH:
   181→1. Granted to the identity via `can_use` edge (tool registry level)
   182→2. Covered by an authorized intent's `authorization_details` (runtime level)
   183→
   184→Ungated tools (policy says no intent required for the action class) skip the intent check and are callable if granted.
   185→
   186→---
   187→
   188→## Integration Points
   189→
   190→### Reused Existing Components
   191→
   192→| Component | File | Reuse Type |
   193→|-----------|------|-----------|
   194→| Proxy auth | `proxy/proxy-auth.ts` | Direct -- resolveProxyAuth gives session + identity |
   195→| RAR verifier | `oauth/rar-verifier.ts` | Direct -- verifyOperationScope for constraint enforcement |
   196→| Intent lifecycle | `intent/status-machine.ts`, `intent-queries.ts` | Direct -- createIntent, updateIntentStatus |
   197→| Intent authorizer | `intent/authorizer.ts` | Direct -- evaluateIntent for policy + LLM eval |
   198→| Policy gate | `policy/policy-gate.ts` | Direct -- evaluatePolicyGate for rule matching |
   199→| MCP client factory | `tool-registry/mcp-client.ts` | Direct -- connect + callTool for upstream forwarding |
   200→| Tool resolver | `proxy/tool-resolver.ts` | Direct -- resolveToolsForIdentity for can_use grants |
   201→| Trace writer | `proxy/tool-trace-writer.ts` | Extended -- captureToolTrace with intent linkage |
   202→| Sandbox adapter | `orchestrator/sandbox-adapter.ts` | Direct -- adapter.resumeSession for observer resume |
   203→| Governance feed | `feed/` | Extended -- new feed card type for MCP intent approval |
   204→
   205→### New Components
   206→
   207→| Component | Location | Purpose |
   208→|-----------|----------|---------|
   209→| Agent MCP route | `mcp/agent-mcp-route.ts` | HTTP handler for `/mcp/agent/:sessionName` |
   210→| Agent MCP auth | `mcp/agent-mcp-auth.ts` | Proxy token to session resolution |
   211→| Scope engine | `mcp/scope-engine.ts` | Effective scope computation from gates edges |
   212→| Tools list handler | `mcp/tools-list-handler.ts` | MCP ListToolsResult builder |
   213→| Tools call handler | `mcp/tools-call-handler.ts` | Scope check, constraint validation, upstream forwarding |
   214→| Create intent handler | `mcp/create-intent-handler.ts` | Intent creation and policy evaluation |
   215→| Error response builder | `mcp/error-response-builder.ts` | Structured 403 with action_spec_template |
   216→
   217→---
   218→
   219→## Quality Attribute Strategies
   220→
   221→### Auditability (Priority 1)
   222→
   223→Every `tools/call` -- success, failure, rejected, or constraint-violated -- produces a trace record in SurrealDB linked to the session and authorizing intent. The trace includes tool name, arguments, result, duration, and outcome. This uses the existing `captureToolTrace` pattern from `proxy/tool-trace-writer.ts`.
   224→
   225→### Security (Priority 2)
   226→
   227→- **Auth**: Proxy token resolves to workspace + identity + session. Invalid/expired/revoked tokens return 401.
   228→- **Scope**: Effective scope is the intersection of can_use grants and authorized intent authorization_details.
   229→- **Constraints**: RAR verifier enforces numeric bounds and string identity constraints before upstream forwarding.
   230→- **Isolation**: Each sandbox agent has its own session; scope is per-session, not per-identity.
   231→
   232→### Maintainability (Priority 3)
   233→
   234→- Pure core / effect shell: scope computation, constraint verification, tool classification are pure functions. IO (DB queries, upstream calls, trace writes) happens at boundaries.
   235→- Composition over inheritance: handlers compose existing modules via function calls.
   236→- No new auth mechanism: reuses proxy-auth for sandbox agents, same pattern as existing proxy pipeline.
   237→
   238→### Testability (Priority 4)
   239→
   240→- All IO is injectable: SurrealDB queries via driven ports, upstream MCP via McpClientFactory, adapter via SandboxAgentAdapter.
   241→- Pure functions (scope computation, constraint verification, tool classification) are unit-testable.
   242→- Acceptance tests follow existing acceptance-test-kit pattern with isolated DB namespace.
   243→
   244→### Time-to-Market (Priority 5)
   245→
   246→- Walking skeleton (US-01 through US-03) reuses ~90% existing infrastructure.
   247→- Release 1 (yield-and-resume) adds observer scan pattern using existing observer infrastructure.
   248→- Release 2 (constraints + composites) reuses RAR verifier directly.
   249→- Release 3 (hardening) is incremental improvements.
   250→
   251→---
   252→
   253→## Deployment Architecture
   254→
   255→No deployment changes. The Agent MCP endpoint is a new route registered in `start-server.ts`, running in the same Bun process as all other Brain routes. SurrealDB schema changes are applied via `bun migrate`.
   256→
   257→---
   258→
   259→## External Integrations
   260→
   261→The Agent MCP endpoint forwards tool calls to upstream MCP servers (GitHub, Stripe, Jira, etc.). These are external integrations accessed through the existing `McpClientFactory`.
   262→
   263→**Contract tests recommended for upstream MCP servers** -- consumer-driven contracts (e.g., Pact-JS) to detect breaking changes in upstream MCP server responses before production. Priority targets: any upstream server where tool calls have financial or destructive side effects.
   264→
   265→---
   266→
   267→## Architectural Enforcement
   268→
   269→Recommended tooling for enforcing the architecture:
   270→
   271→- **Import linting**: Use `dependency-cruiser` (MIT, well-maintained) to enforce:
   272→  - `mcp/scope-engine.ts` must NOT import from `http/` or `runtime/` (pure module)
   273→  - `mcp/agent-mcp-route.ts` is the only file that imports from `mcp/tools-*-handler.ts`
   274→  - No circular dependencies within `mcp/` modules
   275→- **Test coverage gate**: Acceptance tests for every handler; unit tests for every pure function.
   276→
```

```
     1→# Component Boundaries: Intent-Gated MCP Tool Access
     2→
     3→## Module Map
     4→
     5→All new modules live under `app/src/server/mcp/`. Each module has a single responsibility, clear dependencies, and explicit types.
     6→
     7→```
     8→app/src/server/mcp/
     9→  agent-mcp-route.ts        # Route handler (effect boundary)
    10→  agent-mcp-auth.ts         # Proxy token -> session resolution (effect boundary)
    11→  scope-engine.ts           # Effective scope computation (pure core + query port)
    12→  tools-list-handler.ts     # tools/list response builder (pure core)
    13→  tools-call-handler.ts     # tools/call orchestration (effect boundary)
    14→  create-intent-handler.ts  # create_intent MCP tool (effect boundary)
    15→  error-response-builder.ts # Structured 403 builder (pure)
    16→```
    17→
    18→---
    19→
    20→## Module 1: agent-mcp-route.ts
    21→
    22→**Responsibility**: HTTP route handler for `POST /mcp/agent/:sessionName`. Parses JSON-RPC envelope, authenticates via proxy token, dispatches to the appropriate handler based on `method` field.
    23→
    24→**Dependencies**:
    25→- `agent-mcp-auth.ts` (authentication)
    26→- `tools-list-handler.ts` (tools/list dispatch)
    27→- `tools-call-handler.ts` (tools/call dispatch)
    28→- `create-intent-handler.ts` (create_intent dispatch)
    29→- `http/response.ts` (JSON response helpers)
    30→- `http/instrumentation.ts` (withTracing)
    31→
    32→**Type signatures** (ports):
    33→
    34→```typescript
    35→type AgentMcpRouteDeps = {
    36→  readonly surreal: Surreal;
    37→  readonly mcpClientFactory: McpClientFactory;
    38→  readonly toolEncryptionKey: string;
    39→  readonly llmEvaluator: LlmEvaluator;
    40→  readonly inflight: InflightTracker;
    41→};
    42→
    43→// Route registration: called from start-server.ts
    44→type RegisterAgentMcpRoute = (deps: AgentMcpRouteDeps) => RouteHandler;
    45→```
    46→
    47→**Effect boundary**: This is where IO happens (HTTP request parsing, response writing, tracing span management).
    48→
    49→---
    50→
    51→## Module 2: agent-mcp-auth.ts
    52→
    53→**Responsibility**: Resolve a proxy token from `X-Brain-Auth` header into a fully resolved session context (workspace, identity, session ID, session record). Fail fast on invalid/expired tokens or missing sessions.
    54→
    55→**Dependencies**:
    56→- `proxy/proxy-auth.ts` (resolveProxyAuth, LookupProxyToken)
    57→- SurrealDB (session record lookup)
    58→
    59→**Type signatures**:
    60→
    61→```typescript
    62→type AgentSessionContext = {
    63→  readonly workspaceId: string;
    64→  readonly identityId: string;
    65→  readonly sessionId: string;
    66→  readonly sessionRecord: RecordId<"agent_session", string>;
    67→  readonly workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
    68→  readonly identityRecord: RecordId<"identity", string>;
    69→};
    70→
    71→type ResolveAgentSession = (
    72→  headers: Headers,
    73→  lookupToken=[REDACTED],
    74→  tokenCache: TokenCache,
    75→  surreal: Surreal,
    76→) => Promise<AgentSessionContext>;  // throws on auth failure
    77→```
    78→
    79→**Effect boundary**: DB read for session record. Auth errors throw (caught by route handler).
    80→
    81→---
    82→
    83→## Module 3: scope-engine.ts
    84→
    85→**Responsibility**: Compute the effective tool scope for a session. Queries `gates` edges to find all authorized intents, unions their `authorization_details`, and classifies each registered tool as authorized, gated, or ungated (Brain-native).
    86→
    87→**Dependencies**:
    88→- `proxy/tool-resolver.ts` (resolveToolsForIdentity for can_use grants)
    89→- `oauth/rar-verifier.ts` (findMatchingAuthorization for scope matching -- pure)
    90→- `oauth/types.ts` (BrainAction)
    91→- `intent/types.ts` (IntentRecord, ActionSpec)
    92→
    93→**Type signatures**:
    94→
    95→```typescript
    96→// --- Query port (injectable) ---
    97→type QuerySessionIntents = (
    98→  sessionRecord: RecordId<"agent_session", string>,
    99→) => Promise<AuthorizedIntentSummary[]>;
   100→
   101→type AuthorizedIntentSummary = {
   102→  readonly intentId: string;
   103→  readonly authorizationDetails: BrainAction[];
   104→};
   105→
   106→// --- Pure types ---
   107→type ToolClassification =
   108→  | { readonly kind: "authorized"; readonly matchingIntent: AuthorizedIntentSummary }
   109→  | { readonly kind: "gated" }
   110→  | { readonly kind: "brain_native" };
   111→
   112→type EffectiveScope = {
   113→  readonly authorizedActions: BrainAction[];
   114→  readonly intents: AuthorizedIntentSummary[];
   115→};
   116→
   117→type ClassifiedTool = {
   118→  readonly tool: ResolvedTool;
   119→  readonly classification: ToolClassification;
   120→};
   121→
   122→// --- Pure functions ---
   123→type ComputeEffectiveScope = (
   124→  intents: AuthorizedIntentSummary[],
   125→) => EffectiveScope;
   126→
   127→type ClassifyTools = (
   128→  grantedTools: ResolvedTool[],
   129→  effectiveScope: EffectiveScope,
   130→  brainNativeToolNames: ReadonlySet<string>,
   131→) => ClassifiedTool[];
   132→
   133→// --- Effect boundary (composition root) ---
   134→type ResolveSessionScope = (
   135→  sessionRecord: RecordId<"agent_session", string>,
   136→  identityId: string,
   137→  workspaceId: string,
   138→  queryIntents: QuerySessionIntents,
   139→  queryGrantedTools: QueryGrantedTools,
   140→  toolResolutionCache: ToolResolutionCache,
   141→) => Promise<{
   142→  scope: EffectiveScope;
   143→  classifiedTools: ClassifiedTool[];
   144→}>;
   145→```
   146→
   147→**Pure core**: `computeEffectiveScope` and `classifyTools` are pure functions operating on data. `resolveSessionScope` is the composition root with DB queries at the boundary.
   148→
   149→---
   150→
   151→## Module 4: tools-list-handler.ts
   152→
   153→**Responsibility**: Build an MCP `ListToolsResult` response from classified tools. Authorized tools get their full definition. Gated tools get enriched descriptions instructing the agent to call `create_intent`. Brain-native tools (create_intent, get_context) are always included.
   154→
   155→**Dependencies**:
   156→- `scope-engine.ts` (ClassifiedTool)
   157→- `error-response-builder.ts` (gated tool description enrichment)
   158→
   159→**Type signatures**:
   160→
   161→```typescript
   162→type McpToolDefinition = {
   163→  readonly name: string;
   164→  readonly description: string;
   165→  readonly inputSchema: Record<string, unknown>;
   166→};
   167→
   168→type ListToolsResult = {
   169→  readonly tools: McpToolDefinition[];
   170→};
   171→
   172→// Pure function
   173→type BuildToolsList = (
   174→  classifiedTools: ClassifiedTool[],
   175→  brainNativeTools: McpToolDefinition[],
   176→) => ListToolsResult;
   177→```
   178→
   179→**Pure**: No IO. Transforms classified tools into MCP protocol response.
   180→
   181→---
   182→
   183→## Module 5: tools-call-handler.ts
   184→
   185→**Responsibility**: Orchestrate a tool call: check scope, validate constraints, forward to upstream MCP server, record trace. Returns MCP `CallToolResult` on success, structured error on failure.
   186→
   187→**Dependencies**:
   188→- `scope-engine.ts` (EffectiveScope)
   189→- `oauth/rar-verifier.ts` (verifyOperationScope, findExceededConstraint)
   190→- `tool-registry/mcp-client.ts` (McpClientFactory)
   191→- `proxy/tool-trace-writer.ts` (captureToolTrace)
   192→- `error-response-builder.ts` (403 responses)
   193→- `tool-registry/server-queries.ts` (getMcpServerById for upstream lookup)
   194→- `proxy/credential-resolver.ts` (resolveCredentialsForTool)
   195→
   196→**Type signatures**:
   197→
   198→```typescript
   199→type ToolCallInput = {
   200→  readonly toolName: string;
   201→  readonly arguments: Record<string, unknown>;
   202→  readonly requestId: string | number;
   203→};
   204→
   205→type ToolCallOutcome =
   206→  | { readonly kind: "success"; readonly result: unknown }
   207→  | { readonly kind: "intent_required"; readonly toolName: string; readonly actionSpecTemplate: ActionSpecTemplate }
   208→  | { readonly kind: "constraint_violated"; readonly field: string; readonly requested: unknown; readonly authorized: unknown }
   209→  | { readonly kind: "upstream_error"; readonly error: string; readonly statusCode?: number }
   210→  | { readonly kind: "timeout" };
   211→
   212→type ActionSpecTemplate = {
   213→  readonly provider: string;
   214→  readonly action: string;
   215→  readonly parameterSchema?: Record<string, unknown>;
   216→};
   217→
   218→// Effect boundary
   219→type HandleToolCall = (
   220→  input: ToolCallInput,
   221→  sessionContext: AgentSessionContext,
   222→  scope: EffectiveScope,
   223→  grantedTools: ResolvedTool[],
   224→  deps: ToolCallDeps,
   225→) => Promise<ToolCallOutcome>;
   226→
   227→type ToolCallDeps = {
   228→  readonly surreal: Surreal;
   229→  readonly mcpClientFactory: McpClientFactory;
   230→  readonly toolEncryptionKey: string;
   231→};
   232→```
   233→
   234→**Effect boundary**: Upstream MCP call, trace write, credential resolution.
   235→
   236→---
   237→
   238→## Module 6: create-intent-handler.ts
   239→
   240→**Responsibility**: Handle the `create_intent` Brain-native MCP tool. Creates an intent record, submits it through the policy gate and LLM evaluator, creates a `gates` edge linking the intent to the session, and returns the outcome.
   241→
   242→**Dependencies**:
   243→- `intent/intent-queries.ts` (createIntent, updateIntentStatus)
   244→- `intent/authorizer.ts` (evaluateIntent)
   245→- `intent/status-machine.ts` (transitionStatus)
   246→- `policy/policy-gate.ts` (evaluatePolicyGate)
   247→- `oauth/types.ts` (BrainAction, createBrainAction)
   248→- SurrealDB (RELATE for gates edge)
   249→
   250→**Type signatures**:
   251→
   252→```typescript
   253→type CreateIntentInput = {
   254→  readonly goal: string;
   255→  readonly reasoning: string;
   256→  readonly actionSpec: ActionSpec;
   257→};
   258→
   259→type CreateIntentOutcome =
   260→  | { readonly status: "authorized"; readonly intentId: string }
   261→  | { readonly status: "pending_veto"; readonly intentId: string }
   262→  | { readonly status: "vetoed"; readonly intentId: string; readonly reason: string };
   263→
   264→// Effect boundary
   265→type HandleCreateIntent = (
   266→  input: CreateIntentInput,
   267→  sessionContext: AgentSessionContext,
   268→  deps: CreateIntentDeps,
   269→) => Promise<CreateIntentOutcome>;
   270→
   271→type CreateIntentDeps = {
   272→  readonly surreal: Surreal;
   273→  readonly llmEvaluator: LlmEvaluator;
   274→};
   275→```
   276→
   277→**Effect boundary**: Intent creation (DB write), policy evaluation (DB read), gates edge creation (DB write), LLM evaluation (external call).
   278→
   279→**Gates edge direction**: Per the existing schema `DEFINE TABLE gates TYPE RELATION IN intent OUT agent_session`, the RELATE statement is:
   280→```sql
   281→RELATE $intent->gates->$sess SET created_at = time::now();
   282→```
   283→This matches the existing pattern in `session-lifecycle.ts:transitionIntentToExecuting`.
   284→
   285→---
   286→
   287→## Module 7: error-response-builder.ts
   288→
   289→**Responsibility**: Build structured error responses for the MCP protocol. The primary concern is the `intent_required` 403 response with `action_spec_template` that guides the agent to create an intent.
   290→
   291→**Dependencies**:
   292→- `tool-registry/types.ts` (McpToolRecord for parameter schema)
   293→
   294→**Type signatures**:
   295→
   296→```typescript
   297→type IntentRequiredError = {
   298→  readonly code: -32403;
   299→  readonly message: "intent_required";
   300→  readonly data: {
   301→    readonly tool: string;
   302→    readonly action_spec_template: ActionSpecTemplate;
   303→  };
   304→};
   305→
   306→type ConstraintViolationError = {
   307→  readonly code: -32403;
   308→  readonly message: "constraint_violation";
   309→  readonly data: {
   310→    readonly field: string;
   311→    readonly requested: unknown;
   312→    readonly authorized: unknown;
   313→  };
   314→};
   315→
   316→// Pure functions
   317→type BuildIntentRequiredError = (
   318→  toolName: string,
   319→  toolkit: string,
   320→  parameterSchema?: Record<string, unknown>,
   321→) => IntentRequiredError;
   322→
   323→type BuildConstraintViolationError = (
   324→  field: string,
   325→  requested: unknown,
   326→  authorized: unknown,
   327→) => ConstraintViolationError;
   328→
   329→// Gated tool description enrichment (pure)
   330→type EnrichGatedDescription = (
   331→  originalDescription: string,
   332→  toolkit: string,
   333→  action: string,
   334→) => string;
   335→```
   336→
   337→**Pure**: No IO. String manipulation and object construction only.
   338→
   339→---
   340→
   341→## Dependency Graph
   342→
   343→```
   344→agent-mcp-route
   345→  |-- agent-mcp-auth
   346→  |     |-- proxy/proxy-auth (existing)
   347→  |     |-- SurrealDB (session lookup)
   348→  |
   349→  |-- tools-list-handler (pure)
   350→  |     |-- scope-engine
   351→  |     |-- error-response-builder (pure)
   352→  |
   353→  |-- tools-call-handler
   354→  |     |-- scope-engine
   355→  |     |-- oauth/rar-verifier (existing, pure)
   356→  |     |-- tool-registry/mcp-client (existing)
   357→  |     |-- proxy/tool-trace-writer (existing)
   358→  |     |-- proxy/credential-resolver (existing)
   359→  |     |-- error-response-builder (pure)
   360→  |
   361→  |-- create-intent-handler
   362→        |-- intent/intent-queries (existing)
   363→        |-- intent/authorizer (existing)
   364→        |-- policy/policy-gate (existing)
   365→        |-- oauth/types (existing, pure)
   366→```
   367→
   368→**Inward dependency rule**: Pure modules (`scope-engine` pure functions, `tools-list-handler`, `error-response-builder`) never depend on effect modules. Effect modules depend on pure modules. The route handler is the composition root.
   369→
   370→---
   371→
   372→## Brain-Native Tools for Agent MCP
   373→
   374→The Agent MCP endpoint provides these Brain-native tools to every session:
   375→
   376→| Tool | Description | Always Available |
   377→|------|-------------|-----------------|
   378→| `create_intent` | Create an intent for tool access escalation | Yes |
   379→| `get_context` | Load workspace context (existing MCP tool) | Yes |
   380→| `search_entities` | Search workspace knowledge graph | Yes |
   381→| `get_entity_detail` | Fetch entity with relationships | Yes |
   382→| `create_observation` | Record an observation | Yes |
   383→
   384→These are registered in the tools/list response as non-gated tools. They do not require intent authorization.
   385→
```

     1→# Data Models: Intent-Gated MCP Tool Access
     2→
     3→## Schema Changes
     4→
     5→### No new tables required
     6→
     7→All required tables already exist: `proxy_token`, `agent_session`, `intent`, `gates`, `mcp_tool`, `mcp_server`, `can_use`, `trace`.
     8→
     9→### No schema migration required
    10→
    11→The `proxy_token` table already has `intent` and `session` fields (added in sandbox-agent-integration R2, migration 0065+). The `gates` relation table already exists. No new fields are needed on any existing table.
    12→
    13→---
    14→
    15→## Existing Schema (Relevant Tables)
    16→
    17→### proxy_token
    18→
    19→```sql
    20→DEFINE TABLE proxy_token SCHEMAFULL;
    21→DEFINE FIELD token_hash ON proxy_token TYPE string;
    22→DEFINE FIELD workspace ON proxy_token TYPE record<workspace>;
    23→DEFINE FIELD identity ON proxy_token TYPE record<identity>;
    24→DEFINE FIELD expires_at ON proxy_token TYPE datetime;
    25→DEFINE FIELD created_at ON proxy_token TYPE datetime DEFAULT time::now();
    26→DEFINE FIELD revoked ON proxy_token TYPE bool DEFAULT false;
    27→DEFINE FIELD intent ON proxy_token TYPE option<record<intent>>;      -- initial intent (provenance)
    28→DEFINE FIELD session ON proxy_token TYPE option<record<agent_session>>; -- linked session
    29→```
    30→
    31→**Usage**: Agent MCP endpoint uses `session` field to resolve the agent's session. The `intent` field provides provenance to the initial task-spawning intent (separate from runtime tool intents).
    32→
    33→### gates (relation: intent -> agent_session)
    34→
    35→```sql
    36→DEFINE TABLE gates TYPE RELATION IN intent OUT agent_session SCHEMAFULL;
    37→DEFINE FIELD created_at ON gates TYPE datetime;
    38→```
    39→
    40→**Direction**: `intent -gates-> agent_session`. This means:
    41→- To find all intents for a session: `SELECT in.* FROM gates WHERE out = $session`
    42→- To create a gates edge: `RELATE $intent->gates->$sess SET created_at = time::now();`
    43→
    44→**Usage**: The scope engine queries gates edges to find all authorized intents linked to a session. The create-intent handler creates gates edges when intents are created.
    45→
    46→### intent
    47→
    48→```sql
    49→DEFINE TABLE intent SCHEMAFULL;
    50→DEFINE FIELD goal ON intent TYPE string;
    51→DEFINE FIELD reasoning ON intent TYPE string;
    52→DEFINE FIELD status ON intent TYPE string
    53→  ASSERT $value IN ["draft", "pending_auth", "pending_veto", "authorized", "executing", "completed", "vetoed", "failed"];
    54→DEFINE FIELD priority ON intent TYPE int;
    55→DEFINE FIELD action_spec ON intent TYPE object FLEXIBLE;
    56→-- action_spec fields: provider (string), action (string), params (option<object>)
    57→DEFINE FIELD authorization_details ON intent TYPE option<array<object>> FLEXIBLE;
    58→-- Each element: { type: "brain_action", action: string, resource: string, constraints?: object }
    59→DEFINE FIELD workspace ON intent TYPE record<workspace>;
    60→DEFINE FIELD requester ON intent TYPE record<identity>;
    61→-- ... evaluation, veto, trace fields omitted for brevity
    62→```
    63→
    64→**Usage**: The create-intent handler creates intent records. The scope engine reads `authorization_details` from authorized intents.
    65→
    66→### mcp_tool
    67→
    68→```sql
    69→DEFINE TABLE mcp_tool SCHEMAFULL;
    70→DEFINE FIELD name ON mcp_tool TYPE string;
    71→DEFINE FIELD toolkit ON mcp_tool TYPE string;           -- provider name (e.g., "github", "stripe")
    72→DEFINE FIELD description ON mcp_tool TYPE string;
    73→DEFINE FIELD input_schema ON mcp_tool TYPE object FLEXIBLE;
    74→DEFINE FIELD output_schema ON mcp_tool TYPE option<object> FLEXIBLE;
    75→DEFINE FIELD risk_level ON mcp_tool TYPE string ASSERT $value IN ["low", "medium", "high", "critical"];
    76→DEFINE FIELD workspace ON mcp_tool TYPE record<workspace>;
    77→DEFINE FIELD status ON mcp_tool TYPE string ASSERT $value IN ["active", "disabled"];
    78→DEFINE FIELD source_server ON mcp_tool TYPE option<record<mcp_server>>;
    79→```
    80→
    81→**Usage**: tools/list queries mcp_tool to build the full tool list. The `toolkit` field maps to the `provider` in `action_spec`. The `source_server` field links to the upstream MCP server for forwarding.
    82→
    83→### can_use (relation: identity -> mcp_tool)
    84→
    85→```sql
    86→DEFINE TABLE can_use TYPE RELATION IN identity OUT mcp_tool SCHEMAFULL;
    87→DEFINE FIELD granted_at ON can_use TYPE datetime DEFAULT time::now();
    88→DEFINE FIELD max_calls_per_hour ON can_use TYPE option<int>;
    89→```
    90→
    91→**Usage**: The scope engine queries can_use to determine which tools are granted to the agent's identity. This is the first gate -- a tool must be granted before intent authorization matters.
    92→
    93→### mcp_server
    94→
    95→```sql
    96→DEFINE TABLE mcp_server SCHEMAFULL;
    97→DEFINE FIELD name ON mcp_server TYPE string;
    98→DEFINE FIELD url ON mcp_server TYPE string;
    99→DEFINE FIELD transport ON mcp_server TYPE string DEFAULT "streamable-http";
   100→DEFINE FIELD workspace ON mcp_server TYPE record<workspace>;
   101→DEFINE FIELD status ON mcp_server TYPE string DEFAULT "active";
   102→DEFINE FIELD auth_mode ON mcp_server TYPE string DEFAULT "none";
   103→-- ... credential, discovery fields omitted for brevity
   104→```
   105→
   106→**Usage**: tools/call uses `source_server` on the mcp_tool record to look up the upstream MCP server for forwarding.
   107→
   108→---
   109→
   110→## Query Patterns
   111→
   112→### Q1: Resolve session from proxy token
   113→
   114→Already implemented in `proxy-auth.ts:createLookupProxyToken`.
   115→
   116→```sql
   117→SELECT workspace, identity, expires_at, revoked, intent, session
   118→FROM proxy_token
   119→WHERE token_hash = $hash
   120→LIMIT 1;
   121→```
   122→
   123→### Q2: Load session record
   124→
   125→Already implemented in `session-lifecycle.ts:lookupSession`.
   126→
   127→```sql
   128→SELECT * FROM $sessionRecord;
   129→-- where $sessionRecord = new RecordId("agent_session", sessionId)
   130→```
   131→
   132→### Q3: Load authorized intents for session (scope computation)
   133→
   134→**New query** for scope-engine.ts.
   135→
   136→```sql
   137→SELECT
   138→  in.id AS intent_id,
   139→  in.authorization_details AS authorization_details
   140→FROM gates
   141→WHERE out = $session
   142→  AND in.status = "authorized";
   143→```
   144→
   145→**Note**: The `in` traversal follows the `gates` relation direction (intent -> agent_session). Filtering `in.status = "authorized"` ensures only authorized intents contribute to scope.
   146→
   147→**Bound parameters**:
   148→- `$session`: `RecordId<"agent_session", string>`
   149→
   150→**Result type**:
   151→```typescript
   152→type IntentScopeRow = {
   153→  intent_id: RecordId<"intent", string>;
   154→  authorization_details: BrainAction[] | undefined;
   155→};
   156→```
   157→
   158→### Q4: Load granted tools for identity (can_use resolution)
   159→
   160→Already implemented in `proxy/tool-resolver.ts:createQueryGrantedTools`.
   161→
   162→```sql
   163→SELECT out.name AS name, out.description AS description,
   164→       out.input_schema AS input_schema, out.output_schema AS output_schema,
   165→       out.toolkit AS toolkit, out.risk_level AS risk_level,
   166→       out.source_server.id AS source_server_id
   167→FROM can_use
   168→WHERE in = $identity AND out.status = 'active' AND out.workspace = $workspace;
   169→```
   170→
   171→### Q5: Load all active tools in workspace (for gated tool listing)
   172→
   173→**New query** for tools-list-handler.ts. Required to show gated tools the agent doesn't yet have access to.
   174→
   175→```sql
   176→SELECT name, description, input_schema, toolkit, risk_level, source_server
   177→FROM mcp_tool
   178→WHERE workspace = $workspace AND status = "active";
   179→```
   180→
   181→**Bound parameters**:
   182→- `$workspace`: `RecordId<"workspace", string>`
   183→
   184→### Q6: Create gates edge (intent -> session)
   185→
   186→Already implemented in `session-lifecycle.ts:transitionIntentToExecuting`.
   187→
   188→```sql
   189→RELATE $intent->gates->$sess SET created_at = time::now();
   190→```
   191→
   192→**Bound parameters**:
   193→- `$intent`: `RecordId<"intent", string>`
   194→- `$sess`: `RecordId<"agent_session", string>` (note: `$session` is a SurrealDB protected variable)
   195→
   196→### Q7: Intent dedup check (Release 3)
   197→
   198→Check for existing non-terminal intent with same session + action_spec.
   199→
   200→```sql
   201→SELECT id, status
   202→FROM intent
   203→WHERE workspace = $workspace
   204→  AND id IN (SELECT in FROM gates WHERE out = $session).in
   205→  AND action_spec.provider = $provider
   206→  AND action_spec.action = $action
   207→  AND status NOT IN ["vetoed", "failed", "completed"]
   208→LIMIT 1;
   209→```
   210→
   211→### Q8: Observer scan for authorized intents on idle sessions
   212→
   213→**New query** for observer resume pattern.
   214→
   215→```sql
   216→SELECT
   217→  out.id AS session_id,
   218→  out.external_session_id AS external_session_id,
   219→  in.id AS intent_id,
   220→  in.status AS intent_status,
   221→  in.goal AS intent_goal,
   222→  in.action_spec AS action_spec,
   223→  in.veto_reason AS veto_reason
   224→FROM gates
   225→WHERE out.orchestrator_status = "idle"
   226→  AND in.status IN ["authorized", "vetoed"]
   227→  AND in.updated_at > $last_scan;
   228→```
   229→
   230→**Bound parameters**:
   231→- `$last_scan`: `datetime` (last observer scan timestamp)
   232→
   233→---
   234→
   235→## ActionSpec-to-BrainAction Mapping
   236→
   237→When the agent calls `create_intent`, the `action_spec` is mapped to `authorization_details` (array of `BrainAction`):
   238→
   239→```typescript
   240→// Input from agent
   241→const actionSpec: ActionSpec = {
   242→  provider: "stripe",
   243→  action: "create_refund",
   244→  params: { amount: 5000, currency: "usd" }
   245→};
   246→
   247→// Derived BrainAction for authorization_details
   248→const brainAction: BrainAction = {
   249→  type: "brain_action",
   250→  action: "execute",
   251→  resource: `mcp_tool:${actionSpec.provider}:${actionSpec.action}`,
   252→  constraints: actionSpec.params  // only policy-relevant params become constraints
   253→};
   254→```
   255→
   256→The `resource` field uses a composite identifier: `mcp_tool:<provider>:<action>`. This allows policies to match on provider wildcards (`mcp_tool:stripe:*`) or specific tools (`mcp_tool:stripe:create_refund`).
   257→
   258→For composite intents, the `authorization_details` array contains multiple `BrainAction` entries:
   259→
   260→```typescript
   261→const compositeDetails: BrainAction[] = [
   262→  { type: "brain_action", action: "execute", resource: "mcp_tool:stripe:list_charges" },
   263→  { type: "brain_action", action: "execute", resource: "mcp_tool:stripe:create_refund",
   264→    constraints: { amount: 5000, currency: "usd" } },
   265→];
   266→```
   267→
   268→---
   269→
   270→## Scope Computation Algorithm (Pure)
   271→
   272→```
   273→Input: session gates edges, identity can_use grants
   274→
   275→1. Query gates edges for session -> get authorized intents
   276→2. Flatten all intents' authorization_details -> EffectiveScope.authorizedActions
   277→3. Query can_use grants for identity -> granted tools
   278→4. For each granted tool:
   279→   a. If tool name is in brainNativeToolNames -> classify as "brain_native"
   280→   b. Else, check if any authorizedAction matches the tool (by resource):
   281→      - resource = "mcp_tool:{tool.toolkit}:{tool.name}"
   282→      - If match found -> classify as "authorized" (with matching intent)
   283→      - If no match -> classify as "gated"
   284→5. Additionally, query all workspace mcp_tools NOT in can_use grants:
   285→   - These are also "gated" (agent can see them but needs both grant + intent)
   286→
   287→Output: ClassifiedTool[] for tools/list; EffectiveScope for tools/call
   288→```
   289→
   290→---
   291→
   292→## Trace Record Schema
   293→
   294→Uses the existing trace capture pattern from `proxy/tool-trace-writer.ts`. The `captureToolTrace` function already supports `sessionId` and tool metadata. For intent-gated calls, the trace includes:
   295→
   296→| Field | Type | Description |
   297→|-------|------|-------------|
   298→| tool_name | string | MCP tool name |
   299→| workspace_id | string | Workspace scope |
   300→| identity_id | string | Agent's identity |
   301→| session_id | string | Agent session |
   302→| intent_id | string | Authorizing intent (new field) |
   303→| outcome | string | "success", "error", "timeout", "constraint_violated", "intent_required" |
   304→| duration_ms | number | Total call duration |
   305→| input | object | Tool arguments (sanitized) |
   306→| output | object | Tool result or error (sanitized) |
   307→
   308→The `intent_id` field is a new addition to the trace context, linking each tool call to the specific intent that authorized it. This enables audit queries like "show all tool calls authorized by intent X".
   309→
```

```
     1→# Technology Stack: Intent-Gated MCP Tool Access
     2→
     3→## Principle: Reuse Existing
     4→
     5→This feature introduces zero new technology dependencies. Every component reuses the existing Brain stack.
     6→
     7→---
     8→
     9→## Stack Summary
    10→
    11→| Layer | Technology | License | Rationale |
    12→|-------|-----------|---------|-----------|
    13→| Runtime | Bun (`Bun.serve`) | MIT | Existing Brain runtime. New route registered in `start-server.ts`. |
    14→| Language | TypeScript (strict) | Apache 2.0 | Existing codebase language. Functional paradigm per CLAUDE.md. |
    15→| Database | SurrealDB | BSL 1.1 | Existing graph database. No new tables; reuses `proxy_token`, `gates`, `intent`, `mcp_tool`, `mcp_server`, `can_use`. |
    16→| MCP Client | `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` | MIT | Existing dependency for upstream MCP server communication via `McpClientFactory`. |
    17→| Auth | Proxy token (X-Brain-Auth) | N/A (internal) | Existing auth mechanism from `proxy/proxy-auth.ts`. No DPoP for sandbox agents. |
    18→| Policy | Policy gate + predicate evaluator | N/A (internal) | Existing policy evaluation pipeline from `policy/policy-gate.ts`. |
    19→| LLM Eval | Vercel AI SDK (`ai` package) | Apache 2.0 | Existing dependency for intent risk evaluation via `generateObject`. |
    20→| Tracing | OpenTelemetry | Apache 2.0 | Existing instrumentation via `withTracing` and `captureToolTrace`. |
    21→| Schema | Zod | MIT | Existing validation library for MCP tool input schemas and intent validation. |
    22→
    23→---
    24→
    25→## New Runtime Dependencies
    26→
    27→**None.** All dependencies are already in `package.json`.
    28→
    29→---
    30→
    31→## Architecture Enforcement Tooling
    32→
    33→| Tool | License | Purpose |
    34→|------|---------|---------|
    35→| `dependency-cruiser` | MIT | Import dependency validation. Enforces module boundary rules (pure modules cannot import IO modules). Recommended for CI integration. |
    36→
    37→`dependency-cruiser` is the recommended enforcement tool for this architecture. It can express rules like:
    38→- `scope-engine.ts` pure functions must not import from `surrealdb`, `http/`, or `runtime/`
    39→- `error-response-builder.ts` must not import any IO module
    40→- No circular dependencies within `mcp/` module group
    41→
    42→This is a recommendation for the platform-architect wave, not a requirement for the DESIGN wave.
    43→
```

     1→# Walking Skeletons: Intent-Gated MCP Tool Access
     2→
     3→## Walking Skeleton Identification
     4→
     5→Three walking skeletons that each deliver observable user value end-to-end.
     6→
     7→### WS-1: Agent discovers tools and calls an ungated tool
     8→
     9→**User goal**: Agent discovers which tools are available and successfully calls one it is authorized for.
    10→
    11→**E2E path**: proxy token auth -> session resolution -> scope computation -> tools/list response -> tools/call -> upstream forwarding -> trace record
    12→
    13→**Stakeholder demo**: "The agent connected, saw which tools it could use, called one, and got the result back."
    14→
    15→**Stories**: US-01 (tools/list), US-02 (tools/call)
    16→
    17→### WS-2: Agent escalates for gated tool and calls after auto-approval
    18→
    19→**User goal**: Agent encounters a gated tool, self-escalates by creating an intent, gets auto-approved, and successfully calls the tool.
    20→
    21→**E2E path**: tools/call -> 403 intent_required -> create_intent -> policy gate (auto-approve) -> gates edge -> retry tools/call -> upstream forwarding -> result
    22→
    23→**Stakeholder demo**: "The agent was told it needed permission, asked for it, got approved by policy, and then completed the tool call -- all without human intervention."
    24→
    25→**Stories**: US-01, US-02, US-03
    26→
    27→### WS-3: Agent yields on pending veto and resumes after human approval
    28→
    29→**User goal**: Agent needs a high-risk tool, creates an intent requiring human veto, yields execution, human approves, agent resumes and completes the call.
    30→
    31→**E2E path**: create_intent -> pending_veto -> session idle -> human approve endpoint -> intent authorized -> observer detect -> adapter.resumeSession -> tools/call success
    32→
    33→**Stakeholder demo**: "The agent asked for permission to do something risky, waited for a human to review it, and once approved, automatically resumed and completed the work."
    34→
    35→**Stories**: US-03, US-04, US-05
    36→
    37→## Implementation Sequence
    38→
    39→Enable scenarios in this order (one-at-a-time TDD):
    40→
    41→1. **WS-1** -- validates core auth + scope + tools/list + tools/call pipeline
    42→2. **HP-1** through **HP-3** -- refines tools/list classification logic
    43→3. **EP-1**, **EC-1** -- auth and session error paths
    44→4. **HP-4**, **EC-2** -- tools/call forwarding and trace recording
    45→5. **EP-2**, **EP-3** -- gated tool 403 response
    46→6. **WS-2** -- create_intent auto-approve flow (composes previous scenarios)
    47→7. **HP-5**, **HP-6** -- intent-to-scope propagation
    48→8. **EP-4**, **EP-5** -- policy-denied intent paths
    49→9. **HP-7** -- pending_veto path
    50→10. **HP-8**, **HP-9** -- human approve/veto endpoints
    51→11. **WS-3** -- yield-and-resume (composes veto + observer + resume)
    52→12. **EP-6**, **EP-7**, **EP-8** -- constraint enforcement (R2)
    53→13. **HP-10** -- composite intents (R2)
    54→14. **EC-3** -- intent dedup (R3)
    55→

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain-v1/el-paso-v1/docs/evolution/2026-03-25-intent-gated-mcp.md",
  "content": "# Evolution: Intent-Gated MCP Tool Access\n\n**Date**: 2026-03-25\n**Feature ID**: intent-gated-mcp\n**Duration**: ~2 hours (12:16 - 13:47 UTC)\n**Scope**: Walking Skeleton + R1 (yield-and-resume)\n\n## Summary\n\nImplemented a dynamic per-agent MCP endpoint (`POST /mcp/agent/:sessionName`) that gates external tool calls behind intent authorization and policy evaluation. Sandbox coding agents (Claude Code, Codex) interact with Brain through this endpoint, which computes an effective tool scope from the agent's session-linked intents and either forwards authorized calls to upstream MCP servers or guides the agent through intent-based escalation.\n\n## Business Context\n\nAgents need governed access to external tools (GitHub, Stripe, etc.) without blanket permissions. The intent-gated MCP endpoint enforces the principle that every tool call must be traceable to an authorized intent, enabling:\n\n- **Auditability**: Every tool call produces a trace record linked to the authorizing intent\n- **Policy enforcement**: Workspace policies determine whether intents are auto-approved, require human veto review, or are denied\n- **Agent self-escalation**: Agents discover they need permission, request it via `create_intent`, and retry after approval\n- **Human-in-the-loop**: High-risk operations pause for human approval via the governance feed\n\n## Architecture\n\nSeven new modules under `app/src/server/mcp/`:\n\n| Module | Type | Purpose |\n|--------|------|---------|\n| `agent-mcp-route.ts` | Effect boundary | HTTP route handler, JSON-RPC dispatch |\n| `agent-mcp-auth.ts` | Effect boundary | Proxy token to session resolution |\n| `scope-engine.ts` | Pure core + query port | Effective scope computation from gates edges |\n| `tools-list-handler.ts` | Pure | MCP ListToolsResult builder with tool classification |\n| `tools-call-handler.ts` | Effect boundary | Scope check, upstream forwarding, trace recording |\n| `create-intent-handler.ts` | Effect boundary | Intent creation, policy evaluation, gates edge creation |\n| `error-response-builder.ts` | Pure | Structured 403 with action_spec_template |\n\n**Key design pattern**: Pure core / effect shell. Scope computation, tool classification, and error building are pure functions. DB queries, upstream MCP calls, and trace writes happen at boundaries with injectable dependencies.\n\n**Zero new dependencies**: Reuses existing proxy auth, tool resolver, intent lifecycle, policy gate, MCP client factory, and trace writer.\n\n## Steps Completed\n\n| Step | Name | Phase |\n|------|------|-------|\n| 01-01 | Error response builder and scope engine pure functions | Auth, Scope Engine, Route Shell |\n| 01-02 | Agent MCP auth -- proxy token to session resolution | Auth, Scope Engine, Route Shell |\n| 01-03 | Agent MCP route shell with tools/list handler | Auth, Scope Engine, Route Shell |\n| 02-01 | Tools/call handler -- authorized call forwarding and trace | Tools/call and Trace Recording |\n| 02-02 | 403 intent_required for gated tools | Tools/call and Trace Recording |\n| 02-03 | Scope engine query port -- load authorized intents from gates edges | Tools/call and Trace Recording |\n| 03-01 | Create intent handler -- auto-approve path | Create Intent and Escalation Flow |\n| 03-02 | Policy-denied intent path | Create Intent and Escalation Flow |\n| 03-03 | Full escalation cycle -- WS-2 walking skeleton | Create Intent and Escalation Flow |\n| 04-01 | Pending veto path -- create_intent returns pending_veto | Pending Veto and Yield-and-Resume |\n| 04-02 | Human approve/veto endpoints and WS-3 walking skeleton | Pending Veto and Yield-and-Resume |\n\nAll 11 steps passed through PREPARE -> RED_ACCEPTANCE -> GREEN -> COMMIT phases via TDD.\n\n## Key Design Decisions\n\n1. **No DPoP for sandbox agents** (D1): Sandbox agents authenticate via `X-Brain-Auth` proxy token only. They cannot generate DPoP proofs. The proxy token binds to session and workspace.\n\n2. **Agent-driven escalation via create_intent tool** (D3): Two mechanisms -- proactive (tools/list enriches gated descriptions with instructions) and reactive (tools/call returns structured 403 with action_spec_template).\n\n3. **Yield-and-resume, not polling** (D4): When `create_intent` returns `pending_veto`, the agent yields. The Observer detects the authorized intent on idle sessions and triggers `adapter.resumeSession`.\n\n4. **Intent accumulation via gates edges** (D5): Sessions accumulate intents over their lifetime. `gates` relation (intent -> agent_session) links intents to sessions. Scope computation unions all authorized intents' authorization_details.\n\n5. **Effective scope = can_use INTERSECT intent authorization_details** (D6): A tool must be both granted via `can_use` edge AND covered by an authorized intent.\n\n6. **Single acceptance test file** (DISTILL): `tests/acceptance/agent-mcp-governance.test.ts` with scenarios enabled one-at-a-time via TDD. Mock upstream MCP via `mcpClientFactoryOverride` (no MSW).\n\n## Test Coverage\n\n24 test scenarios designed (3 walking skeletons + 10 happy path + 11 error/edge). 18 implemented in Walking Skeleton + R1 scope:\n\n- **WS-1**: Agent discovers tools and calls an authorized tool (full E2E)\n- **WS-2**: Agent escalates for gated tool via create_intent (full escalation cycle)\n- **WS-3**: Agent yields on pending_veto, human approves, agent resumes\n- **HP-1 through HP-9**: Classification, forwarding, traces, intent flows, human approve/veto\n- **EP-1 through EP-5**: Auth errors, 403 intent_required, policy denial\n- **EC-1, EC-2**: Unknown session 404, trace recording for every call\n\n## Deferred to Follow-up Roadmaps\n\n- **R2**: Constraint enforcement (EP-6/EP-7/EP-8), composite intents (HP-10)\n- **R3**: Intent dedup (EC-3), timeout handling, scope caching\n\n## Lessons Learned\n\n1. **SurrealDB protected variable `$session`**: Must use `$sess` for bound parameters in gates edge queries -- `$session` is reserved in SurrealDB v3.0.\n\n2. **Gates edge direction matters**: `intent -gates-> agent_session` (IN intent OUT agent_session). Queries traverse `SELECT in.* FROM gates WHERE out = $session`, not the reverse.\n\n3. **Functional paradigm pays off**: Pure core functions (scope computation, tool classification, error building) were trivially unit-testable. Effect boundaries (auth, upstream forwarding, trace recording) tested via acceptance tests against real SurrealDB.\n\n## Migrated Artifacts\n\n- Architecture design: `docs/architecture/intent-gated-mcp/`\n- Test scenarios: `docs/scenarios/intent-gated-mcp/`\n"
}
```

## Summary

I've thoroughly mapped the Brain codebase's tools across three distinct sources. Here's the complete picture:

---

### 1. BRAIN-NATIVE TOOLS IN `/app/src/server/tools/` (22 Tool Files)

These are defined in TypeScript and used by the **chat agent**, **PM subagent**, and **observer agent**. They are NOT in the MCP tool registry database, but are baked into the code.

**Tools exported from `createChatAgentTools()` in `tools/index.ts`:**

| Tool Name | File | Purpose |
|-----------|------|---------|
| `list_workspace_entities` | `list-workspace-entities.ts` | List entities by type (tasks, decisions, etc.) |
| `search_entities` | `search-entities.ts` | Full-text BM25 search across knowledge graph |
| `get_entity_detail` | `get-entity-detail.ts` | Fetch full entity with relationships |
| `get_project_status` | `get-project-status.ts` | Aggregated project task/decision/question summary |
| `get_conversation_history` | `get-conversation-history.ts` | Load recent chat messages |
| `resolve_decision` | `resolve-decision.ts` | Mark decision as resolved |
| `check_constraints` | `check-constraints.ts` | Validate decision against constraints |
| `create_provisional_decision` | `create-provisional-decision.ts` | Draft a decision for review |
| `create_question` | `create-question.ts` | Ask an open question |
| `confirm_decision` | `confirm-decision.ts` | Finalize a decision (requires auth) |
| `create_observation` | `create-observation.ts` | Log risk/conflict/signal |
| `create_suggestion` | `create-suggestion.ts` | Propose optimization/opportunity |
| `acknowledge_observation` | `acknowledge-observation.ts` | Mark observation as reviewed |
| `resolve_observation` | `resolve-observation.ts` | Close resolved observation |
| `invoke_pm_agent` | `invoke-pm-agent.ts` | Delegate work planning to PM subagent |
| `invoke_analytics_agent` | `invoke-analytics-agent.ts` | Delegate analytics to analytics subagent |
| `show_relationship_graph` | `show-relationship-graph.ts` | Visualize entity relationships |
| `create_work_item` | `create-work-item.ts` | Create task or feature |
| `edit_work_item` | `edit-work-item.ts` | Modify existing work item |
| `move_items_to_project` | `move-items-to-project.ts` | Reassign work items across projects |
| `suggest_work_items` | `suggest-work-items.ts` | Batch triage & dedup (≥0.97 exact, ≥0.8 merge) |

**Supporting files:**
- `helpers.ts` — shared utility functions
- `types.ts` — `ChatAgentToolDeps`, `ChatToolExecutionContext`

---

### 2. BRAIN-NATIVE TOOLS EXPOSED VIA PROXY (4 Tools)

The **proxy layer** (`/app/src/server/proxy/tool-executor.ts`) registers only a **subset** of chat agent tools as "Brain-native" for external integration tool execution:

| Tool Name | Handler | Status |
|-----------|---------|--------|
| `search_entities` | `searchEntitiesHandler` | Fully integrated |
| `get_entity_detail` | `getEntityDetailHandler` | Fully integrated |
| `get_project_status` | `getProjectStatusHandler` | Fully integrated |
| `list_workspace_entities` | `listWorkspaceEntitiesHandler` | Fully integrated |
| `check_constraints` | `checkConstraintsHandler` | Fully integrated |

**Gap:** The other 17 tools (create, edit, move, graph, subagent, etc.) are **NOT exposed** to the proxy integration layer.

---

### 3. MCP BRAIN-NATIVE TOOLS (2 Tools)

The **MCP endpoint** (`/api/mcp/agent/:sessionId`) exposes exactly 2 Brain-native tools that are **always available** without intent authorization:

| Tool Name | File | Purpose |
|-----------|------|---------|
| `create_intent` | `mcp/create-intent-handler.ts` | Request authorization for gated tools |
| `get_context` | `mcp/tools-list-handler.ts` | Load graph context (decisions, tasks, observations) |

These are defined in `tools-list-handler.ts` as `BRAIN_NATIVE_TOOLS` and are **NOT in the mcp_tool database table**. They are hardcoded into the MCP response.

**How MCP tool discovery works:**
1. Agent calls `/api/mcp/agent/:sessionId/tools/list` (JSON-RPC `tools/list`)
2. MCP route queries `can_use` edges to find tools granted to identity
3. Scope engine classifies each tool as: `authorized`, `gated`, or `brain_native`
4. `buildToolsList()` returns granted tools + 2 hardcoded brain-native tools (`create_intent`, `get_context`)
5. Gated tools include a `[GATED]` prefix directing agent to call `create_intent`

---

### 4. EXTERNAL INTEGRATION TOOLS (Stored in `mcp_tool` Table)

Tools discovered from external MCP servers are stored in the database:

**Schema** (`schema/migrations/0065_mcp_tool_registry.surql`):
- Table: `mcp_tool` 
- Fields: `name`, `toolkit`, `description`, `input_schema`, `output_schema`, `risk_level`, `workspace`, `status` (active/disabled)
- Authorization edge: `can_use` (identity → mcp_tool)
- Governance edge: `governs_tool` (policy → mcp_tool)

**Discovery & Sync:**
- `tool-registry/discovery.ts`: `discoverTools()`, `applySyncActions()` — diff remote MCP servers vs local records
- `tool-registry/server-routes.ts`: HTTP endpoints for server CRUD and tool discovery
- Tools from external servers are classified as "authorized" or "gated" based on intent scope

---

### 5. THE GAP: Unexposed Brain-Native Tools

**17 tools exist in `tools/` but are NOT exposed via MCP or proxy:**

- Decision/question tools: `create_provisional_decision`, `confirm_decision`, `resolve_decision`, `create_question`
- Observation tools: `create_observation`, `acknowledge_observation`, `resolve_observation`
- Suggestion tools: `create_suggestion`
- Work item tools: `create_work_item`, `edit_work_item`, `move_items_to_project`, `suggest_work_items`
- Graph tools: `show_relationship_graph`
- Subagent tools: `invoke_pm_agent`, `invoke_analytics_agent`
- Conversation tools: `get_conversation_history`

These are **only available to the chat agent** (internally via `createChatAgentTools()`), but coding agents via MCP cannot access them. They would need to be:
1. Added to the `brainToolHandlers` registry in `tool-executor.ts` (proxy layer), OR
2. Exposed via intent-based authorization in the MCP endpoint

---

Here's the gap analysis:

**Currently exposed via MCP:** 2 tools (`get_context`, `create_intent`)
**Currently exposed via proxy:** 5 tools (search/read-only: `search_entities`, `get_entity_detail`, `get_project_status`, `list_workspace_entities`, `check_constraints`)

**17 tools missing from MCP:**

| Category | Tools |
|----------|-------|
| Decisions | `create_provisional_decision`, `confirm_decision`, `resolve_decision` |
| Questions | `create_question` |
| Observations | `create_observation`, `acknowledge_observation`, `resolve_observation` |
| Suggestions | `create_suggestion` |
| Work items | `create_work_item`, `edit_work_item`, `move_items_to_project`, `suggest_work_items` |
| Conversation | `get_conversation_history` |
| Graph viz | `show_relationship_graph` |
| Subagents | `invoke_pm_agent`, `invoke_analytics_agent` |

The read-only tools in the proxy layer (`search_entities`, etc.) are also not in the MCP `tools/list` response — they're only available when the proxy executes tools on behalf of an LLM, not when a coding agent calls MCP directly.

**What should the approach be?** A few options:

1. **Add all 17 as brain-native MCP tools** — always available like `get_context`, no intent gating
2. **Gate write tools, expose read tools freely** — read-only tools (search, list, detail, conversation history, graph) as brain-native; write tools (create/edit/resolve) require intent authorization
3. **Selective subset** — skip subagent dispatch (`invoke_pm_agent`, `invoke_analytics_agent`) and UI-specific tools (`show_relationship_graph`) since they don't make sense for coding agents

Option 3 with the gating from option 2 seems most sensible — expose read tools freely, gate write tools, skip UI/subagent tools. What's your preference?
